<a id="qatar-migration-guide"></a>

# Qatar: migration guide

Sources checked up to 8 Nov 2026.

How to go to Qatar from Bangladesh, visa by visa, step by step, for Bangladesh passport holders, as of November 2026. A raised number such as 3 is an official source; N3 is a news, NGO (non-governmental organisation), research or guide source, listed under "Needs an official source"; a year, like N3 (2022), marks a source older than 2025. "Need to know" marks a point no source has yet confirmed. "You'll need:" lists what to have before starting a procedure.

QR is the Qatari riyal; Tk is the Bangladeshi taka (1 lakh is Tk 100,000). A Bangladesh passport holder needs a visa before travelling to Qatar <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>. Laws are quoted in their current text from Al Meezan, the legal portal of Qatar's Ministry of Justice <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2), [3 (2015)](#o3), [4](#o4)</sup>.

<a id="background"></a>

## 1 Background

<a id="in-one-minute"></a>

### 1.1 In one minute

Qatar still takes Bangladeshi workers, but fewer this year: BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training, the Bangladesh government body that registers and clears migrant workers) cleared 53,611 for Qatar from 1 January to 6 November 2026, under half of 2025's 107,598, and Qatar has dropped to third behind Saudi Arabia and Singapore <sup>[5](#o5), [6](#o6)</sup>. A Qatar job starts as a recruitment approval the employer gets from Qatar's Ministry of Labour (MOL) and a work visa the Ministry of Interior (MOI) issues for QR 300, about Tk 10,000 <sup>[7 (2023)](#o7), [8](#o8)</sup>. Whoever controls those visas in Qatar sells them on, and each seller down the chain to the village adds a cut, so a job the law says should cost the worker nothing is sold for Tk 4 to 10 lakh, about QR 12,000 to 29,000 <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2), [N1 (2017)](#n1), [N2 (2022)](#n2), [N3](#n3)</sup>. The only pay the employer must give is the basic wage in the contract signed at the Qatar Visa Center, and the legal floor is QR 1,000 (about Tk 34,000) a month, so that price equals 12 to 29 months of the minimum basic wage before food, money sent home or loan interest <sup>[9 (2020)](#o9), [N4 (2018)](#n4)</sup>. Since 2020 the law lets a worker change employer and leave Qatar without the employer's permission, but the employer, the sponsor or "kafeel", still controls his residence permit, often holds his passport, and can report him as having run away <sup>[10 (2020)](#o10), [11](#o11), [N5 (2021)](#n5), [N6](#n6), [3 (2015)](#o3)</sup>. Unpaid wages are the most common complaint <sup>[N7](#n7)</sup>. Men sold a "free visa", a visa from a company with no job behind it, find no work on arrival and end up in illegal day labour, at risk of arrest and deportation <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>.

<a id="money-in-this-doc"></a>

#### 1.1.1 Money in this doc

QR 1 is about Tk 34: Bangladesh Bank's rate on 19 October 2026 was USD 1 = Tk 123.65, and the riyal is fixed at QR 3.64 to the dollar <sup>[12](#o12), [N9 (2018)](#n9)</sup>. Taka figures are converted at that rate; older prices are given in the taka of their year.

<a id="is-it-open-now-and-who-can-go"></a>

### 1.2 Is it open now, and who can go?

<a id="status"></a>

#### 1.2.1 Status

As of 8 November 2026: Qatar is open to Bangladeshi workers, but recruitment is slower. BMET's clearances from 1 January to 6 November 2026 put the year on course for about 63,000, well below 2025 (yearly figures: see [The size of the flow](#the-size-of-the-flow), below) <sup>[5](#o5), [6](#o6)</sup>. About 97% of those cleared are men <sup>[13](#o13), [5](#o5)</sup>. After Israel and the United States struck Iran on 28 February 2026, Iran hit US bases and strategic installations in Qatar, Kuwait, the UAE and Bahrain <sup>[N10](#n10)</sup>; Qatar extended expired or expiring entry visas by a month from 28 February, which ended on 7 June 2026 <sup>[N11](#n11), [N12](#n12)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Since the February 2026 strikes, have Qatar employers cut new visa requests for Bangladeshis or cancelled visas already issued, and in which trades?
> - Why are 2026 clearances for Qatar running at about 60% of 2025's pace: fewer visas from Qatar, or something on the Bangladesh side?

<a id="the-channels"></a>

#### 1.2.2 The channels

Three Qatar visas take Bangladeshis to work:
- the work visa on company sponsorship, for private companies and government bodies (see [Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker)) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>;
- the domestic-worker visa, sponsored by a household, for maids, house drivers, cooks and gardeners (see [Work visa: domestic worker](#work-visa-domestic-worker)) <sup>[14 (2019)](#o14), [15 (2017)](#o15)</sup>;
- the temporary work visa, for at most six months (see [Temporary work visa and work visit](#temporary-work-visa-and-work-visit)) <sup>[16 (2019)](#o16)</sup>.

BMET clears workers through agencies on a "group" or "single" visa, or clears a man who found his own visa when he applies himself ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 6, item 3: Get BMET clearance (the smart card)](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight-item-3)) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. BMET publishes almost no Qatar permission letters (its approval for an agency to recruit for a job order), so a worker can't check his agency's Qatar approval online <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>; the agencies with posted Qatar letters are listed in Work visa: company worker ([Step 1, item 3: Check the agency's licence](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3)). BOESL (Bangladesh Overseas Employment and Services Ltd, the government-owned recruiting agency) lists no Qatar recruitment <sup>[19](#o19)</sup>.

<a id="what-the-visa-says-decides-your-rights"></a>

#### 1.2.3 What the visa says decides your rights

Every visa is for a named trade and a named sponsor <sup>[7 (2023)](#o7)</sup>. A visa sponsored by a person, not a company, puts you under the Domestic Workers Law, not the Labour Law, and work for anyone else is illegal (see [What ties you to the employer](#what-ties-you-to-the-employer)) <sup>[4](#o4), [15 (2017)](#o15), [3 (2015)](#o3)</sup>. Many Bangladeshi men on Qatar farms came on domestic-worker visas and were moved to farms, where they work seven days a week on about QR 1,000 <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>. How to read the trade and sponsor on your visa is in Work visa: company worker ([Step 5: Check the visa yourself](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself)).

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshi men go to Qatar each year on household visas (house driver, cook, gardener) rather than company visas?

<a id="which-channel-most-people-use"></a>

#### 1.2.4 Which channel most people use

In a 2025 study by OKUP (Ovibashi Kormi Unnayan Programme, a migrant workers' organisation) of 1,084 migrants to five Gulf countries, Qatar among them, 56% relied on informal or illegal agents and only 7% on licensed agencies <sup>[N14](#n14), [N15](#n15)</sup>. 51% travelled knowingly on a so-called free visa, and 39% of those went through relatives (8% close family, 31% distant relatives) <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. The Bangladesh Bureau of Statistics' 2023 survey of migrants to all countries found 52% paying brokers and only 2.8% helped by relatives or friends <sup>[N16 (2024)](#n16)</sup>; the relative and the broker are often the same person <sup>[N16 (2024)](#n16)</sup>. The relative's route is in Work visa: company worker ([Step 1, item 5: If a relative or friend in Qatar sends you a visa](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-5)).

> **Need to know**
> - What share of Bangladeshis going to Qatar get their visa through a relative already working there, through a village broker, or straight from an agency?
> - What share of BMET's Qatar clearances in 2025 and 2026 were single visas, group visas and visas the worker got himself?

<a id="who-qualifies"></a>

#### 1.2.5 Who qualifies

MOL fixes the gender, number and trade of every recruitment it approves <sup>[7 (2023)](#o7)</sup>. Women were 2,348 of those cleared in 2025 and 1,375 from 1 January to 6 November 2026 <sup>[20](#o20), [13](#o13)</sup>. A domestic worker must be 18 to 60 under Qatari law, and a Bangladeshi woman 25 to 45 (returnees up to 50) when she enrols in the compulsory housekeeping course <sup>[15 (2017)](#o15), [21](#o21)</sup>. A 2026 amendment lets MOL require a training-and-test certificate for the trades it lists ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 1, item 2: Learn a trade if you need one](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-2)) <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - At what minimum and maximum age does BMET clear a man for Qatar?
> - Does Qatar limit work visas by age?
> - Does any Qatar job now need a skill test before the visa, at a TTC (Technical Training Centre), at the Qatar Visa Center or on arrival?

<a id="where-the-workers-come-from-and-what-they-do"></a>

#### 1.2.6 Where the workers come from and what they do

In 2025 Chattogram division sent 36% of the workers cleared for Qatar and Sylhet division 20.5%, against 8.2% of all Bangladeshi workers from Sylhet <sup>[22](#o22), [23](#o23)</sup>. About 473,000 Bangladeshis work in Qatar, 30% in development projects, Qatar's Labour Minister said in May 2026 <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>. On 18 May 2026 he asked for electricians, plumbers, AC (air-conditioning) technicians and welders from 5 named TTCs ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 1, item 2: Learn a trade if you need one](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-2)) <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>. MOL sends every approved recruitment through a "nationalities selection system", which decides how many visas go to each nationality, before MOI issues the visa <sup>[7 (2023)](#o7)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In 2025-26, which trades are most Bangladeshis hired into in Qatar (cleaning, security, facility management, hospitality, labour supply, construction), and in what shares?
> - Is a ceiling on Bangladeshi visa approvals in force now?

<a id="the-size-of-the-flow"></a>

#### 1.2.7 The size of the flow

From 2004 to May 2025, 924,881 workers went to Qatar, 6.63% of all Bangladeshi overseas employment (Saudi Arabia 34.49%, the UAE 15.82%, Oman 11.85%, Malaysia 9.30%) <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>. In 2025 Qatar was BMET's second destination after Saudi Arabia (755,194) of 1,132,360 workers cleared in all <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. India counts 830,491 of its citizens in Qatar as of January 2026, against the 473,000 Bangladeshi workers above <sup>[N17](#n17), [24](#o24)</sup>. Workers BMET cleared for Qatar, by year:
- 2014: 87,575 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>
- 2015: 123,965 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>
- 2016: 120,382 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>
- 2017: 82,012 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>
- 2018: 76,560 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>
- 2019: 50,292 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>
- 2020: 3,608 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>
- 2021: 11,158 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>
- 2022: 24,447 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>
- 2023: 56,148 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>
- 2024: 74,422 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>
- 2025: 107,598 <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>
- 2026, to 6 November: 53,611 <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>

> **Need to know**
> - Is there an official Qatari count of workers by nationality, to compare Bangladeshis with Indians, Nepalis and others?

<a id="what-has-closed-it-before"></a>

#### 1.2.8 What has closed it before
- *June 2017, the Gulf blockade.* After Saudi Arabia, Bahrain and the UAE cut ties with Qatar, departures fell about 31% in January to October 2017 <sup>[N18 (2017)](#n18)</sup>.
- *February 2020.* Bangladesh's expatriates' welfare secretary said Qatar had stopped approving visas for Bangladeshis for six months to balance its foreign-worker quotas and would not hire semi-skilled Bangladeshis <sup>[N19 (2020)](#n19)</sup>; clearances fell to 3,608 that year <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>.
- *October 2025, a rumour.* The Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare called reports that Qatar had stopped visas for Bangladeshis false, said no "free visa" category exists in Qatar, and said 111,662 Bangladeshi workers went to Qatar from September 2024 to September 2025 <sup>[N20](#n20), [N21](#n21), [N22](#n22)</sup>.

<a id="where-it-may-grow"></a>

#### 1.2.9 Where it may grow

The two governments signed a labour cooperation memorandum of understanding (MoU) during the Amir of Qatar's Dhaka visit in April 2024, and a 2025 agreement opened the deployment of Bangladeshi personnel to the Qatar Armed Forces <sup>[N23 (2024)](#n23), [26](#o26)</sup>. MOL's 2024-2030 strategy aims to raise the share of Qataris in private and joint-sector jobs from 17% to 20% and the share of high-skilled workers among expatriates from 20% to 24% <sup>[27 (2024)](#o27)</sup>.

<a id="how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut"></a>

### 1.3 How a job reaches you, and who takes a cut

A Qatar work visa starts as an approval the employer gets from MOL and a QR 300 visa from MOI, and reaches a Bangladeshi village as a "visa" sold for lakhs of taka, because whoever holds it in Qatar, the Dhaka agencies and the village sub-agents each sell it on <sup>[7 (2023)](#o7), [8](#o8), [N1 (2017)](#n1)</sup>. The choke point is the employer's block of visa approvals: whoever controls it, the company, its HR staff or PRO (the clerk who handles its government paperwork), or a broker it deals with, decides which agency gets the demand letter (the employer's written request for workers) <sup>[N1 (2017)](#n1)</sup>.

<a id="who-sells-the-visas"></a>

#### 1.3.1 Who sells the visas

Buying or selling a visa is a crime in both Qatar and Bangladesh <sup>[3 (2015)](#o3), [28](#o28)</sup>. Three kinds of seller stand at the top:
- *Real companies whose staff sell.* Employers or their agents in Qatar demand about USD 300 to 500 (Tk 37,000 to 62,000) per worker from Bangladeshi agencies <sup>[N2 (2022)](#n2)</sup>. In 2022 a Dhaka agency owner said other agencies were buying each Qatar visa for QR 5,000 to 6,000 (about Tk 1.7 to 2 lakh today) <sup>[N2 (2022)](#n2)</sup>.
- *Companies set up to sell visas.* In June 2023 MOI arrested nine people who had set up fake companies to trade visas; police seized the bank cards of the workers who had bought them <sup>[N24 (2023)](#n24)</sup>. Workers on these visas find a company name on paper and no work <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>. In 2018 a company that needed 40 workers got 100 visas; a new company now gets at most 10 visa approvals on its first request <sup>[N25 (2018)](#n25), [29 (2022)](#o29)</sup>.
- *Labour supply companies,* which hire workers out to other firms at the bottom of long subcontracting chains; recruiters often tell workers they are direct hires <sup>[N26](#n26)</sup>.

<a id="down-the-chain"></a>

#### 1.3.2 Down the chain

A broker in the Gulf gets the visas from the employer and sells them to a recruiter in Bangladesh; a demand letter may be resold several times, each seller adding a cut <sup>[N1 (2017)](#n1)</sup>. The Bangladesh Embassy in Doha attests the demand letter and visa, and BMET then issues the agency a permission letter ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 1, item 4: Check the job, the demand letter and the employer](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4)) <sup>[30](#o30), [31 (2021)](#o31), [32](#o32)</sup>. Village sub-agents, mostly unregistered, collect the workers; families pay someone they know because he can be made to refund if the job fails <sup>[N1 (2017)](#n1), [N16 (2024)](#n16)</sup>. A 2023 law makes acting as an unregistered sub-agent an offence, but no public register exists to check one against ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 1, item 3: Check the agency's licence](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3)) <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does a Qatar company-visa demand letter sell for in Dhaka in 2026?
> - How much of the worker's price does the village sub-agent keep?
> - Has any Qatari sponsor been convicted of visa trading?

<a id="the-qatar-visa-center-qvc"></a>

#### 1.3.3 The Qatar Visa Center (QVC)

Before the visa is final the worker goes to QVC, which works for MOI and is run by Biomet Services Pte. Ltd. through its local operator, SQ Services Ltd <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>; there he signs the employment contract, gives fingerprints and does the medical, and the contract shown there is the one that counts ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 4, item 3: At the centre](#step-4-qatar-visa-center-contract-fingerprints-and-medical-item-3)) <sup>[35](#o35), [N4 (2018)](#n4), [36](#o36)</sup>. In May 2026 Bangladesh's minister said there is one QVC and medical centre, in Dhaka, so men from Sylhet and Chattogram, over half the flow, pay for travel and lodging there <sup>[N27](#n27), [22](#o22)</sup>. The worker is not given the medical report: the employer is told "unfit" and the visa is cancelled, nobody at QVC can change the result, and a new visa means paying the visa fee again; the common reasons are in Work visa: company worker ([Step 4, item 6: Track your application](#step-4-qatar-visa-center-contract-fingerprints-and-medical-item-6)) <sup>[N28 (2023)](#n28), [N29 (2023)](#n29), [N30](#n30), [N31 (2022)](#n31)</sup>. After arrival there is a second medical, at Qatar's Medical Commission, before the residence permit (RP) is issued <sup>[37](#o37), [38 (2021)](#o38)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does QVC Sylhet take work-visa appointments in 2026, as QVC's site says?
> - Has any other visa or medical centre opened outside Dhaka?
> - Who pays QVC's fees on a Qatar company visa in practice, the employer or the worker through the agent?
> - After an "unfit" result at QVC, does the agency refund anything, and how much?
> - What happens to a worker who fails the Medical Commission test in Qatar: sent home at whose cost, and with any refund?
> - Do agents still take a "medical fee" before any visa number exists?

<a id="what-changes-when-the-employer-pays"></a>

#### 1.3.4 What changes when the employer pays

From 2018 to 2021 MOL and the ILO (International Labour Organization) ran a fair-recruitment pilot from Bangladesh to Qatar's construction sector with STS, a service provider of the construction company QDVC, and Bonanza Overseas, the Bangladeshi agency recruiting for STS <sup>[39 (2021)](#o39), [N32 (2022)](#n32)</sup>. Workers' average migration cost fell 92% <sup>[39 (2021)](#o39), [40](#o40)</sup>: once the employer paid the agency, workers' payments to the agency disappeared and payments to sub-agents fell 94% <sup>[N32 (2022)](#n32)</sup>. What that agency charges now is in Work visa: company worker ([Step 1, item 3: Check the agency's licence](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3)).

> **Need to know**
> - Which Bangladeshi agencies recruit for Qatar on employer-pays terms in 2026?

<a id="domestic-workers-come-by-a-different-route"></a>

#### 1.3.5 Domestic workers come by a different route

A Qatari household recruits through one of MOL's 221 licensed recruitment offices and pays it up to QR 14,000 (about Tk 4.8 lakh) for a worker from Bangladesh, the January 2022 cap <sup>[N33](#n33), [41 (2022)](#o41)</sup>. In BMET's one posted Qatar permission letter, for 100 housemaids, the employer bears the migration cost <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>. For the steps, see [Work visa: domestic worker](#work-visa-domestic-worker).

> **Need to know**
> - What do Bangladeshi women pay agencies and sub-agents for a Qatar housemaid job in 2026?

<a id="why-a-genuine-visa-can-still-be-a-bad-one"></a>

#### 1.3.6 Why a genuine visa can still be a bad one

A visa sold as a free visa also shows as genuine on MOI's Visa Inquiry, so the check proves only that the visa exists <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>. The checks of sponsor, trade and wage before paying are in Work visa: company worker ([Step 1, item 5: If a relative or friend in Qatar sends you a visa](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-5); [Step 5: Check the visa yourself](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself)).

<a id="the-trade-on-the-visa"></a>

#### 1.3.7 The trade on the visa

A man sold a driver's job needs a Qatar licence, and the trades barred from getting one include builder, plumber, electric welder, installation electrician, cook, waiter, security guard, watchman, gardener and farm worker; the list is not published, and the trade on the QID (Qatar ID card) is printed in Arabic <sup>[N35](#n35), [N36](#n36)</sup>.

<a id="what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep"></a>

### 1.4 What you pay, and what you earn and keep

By law a company worker should pay a Qatari recruiter nothing, and Bangladesh's maximum for Qatar, last reported in 2018, is Tk 1,00,780, about QR 3,000 (see [Before paying: what the law allows](#before-paying-what-the-law-allows); see [The Bangladesh maximum](#the-bangladesh-maximum)) <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2), [N37 (2018)](#n37)</sup>. Men are now quoted Tk 4 to 10 lakh, about QR 12,000 to 29,000, often borrowed at high interest or raised by selling land <sup>[N3](#n3), [N38](#n38), [N39 (2022)](#n39)</sup>.

<a id="what-people-really-pay"></a>

#### 1.4.1 What people really pay
- Prices quoted in Bangladesh now: Tk 4 to 10 lakh for low-skilled Gulf jobs, Qatar among them (December 2025) <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>; Tk 4 to 6 lakh for Qatar visas processed without checking the employer, contract or pay (June 2026) <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>; Tk 4 to 7 lakh, about QR 12,000 to 21,000, for a Qatar "free visa" package (May 2026) <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>.
- On World Cup building sites in 2021, 83% of newly recruited Bangladeshis had paid fees, USD 3,136 (about Tk 3.9 lakh today) on average, the highest of any nationality <sup>[N40 (2021)](#n40)</sup>.
- Men who went to Qatar in 2015 to 2018 paid Tk 4.21 lakh on average and needed about 18 months of their income in Qatar to recover it <sup>[N41 (2022)](#n41), [42](#o42)</sup>.

<a id="how-they-pay"></a>

#### 1.4.2 How they pay

Payment for a traded visa is usually in cash, outside the banks <sup>[N2 (2022)](#n2)</sup>, and in stages: one gang selling fake Qatar jobs took Tk 3 to 4 lakh each in stages and showed fake offer letters, contracts and even a fake visa (2019) <sup>[N42 (2019)](#n42)</sup>. 58% of migrants borrowed to go, in a 2023 survey of all destinations <sup>[N16 (2024)](#n16)</sup>. One Qatar migrant borrowed Tk 3 lakh at Tk 15 per Tk 1,000 a week, about 78% a year, was fired after seven months and came home owing Tk 5 lakh <sup>[N41 (2022)](#n41)</sup>. At that rate, interest on Tk 5 lakh is about Tk 32,000 a month, roughly the whole QR 1,000 basic wage <sup>[N41 (2022)](#n41), [9 (2020)](#o9), [12](#o12), [N9 (2018)](#n9)</sup>. The legal alternatives, a PKB (Probashi Kallyan Bank) loan of up to Tk 3 lakh at 9% and paying the agency by bank, are in Work visa: company worker ([Step 6, item 5: If you need a loan](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight-item-5); see [How to pay](#how-to-pay)) <sup>[43](#o43), [44](#o44)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In 2026, what are the usual payment stages for a Qatar visa (deposit, on the visa copy, at QVC, at the ticket or BMET card), and how much is paid at each?
> - Does the agent usually keep the worker's passport from the first payment until the flight?

<a id="what-the-job-pays"></a>

#### 1.4.3 What the job pays

The floor, since March 2021 and for domestic workers too, is QR 1,000 a month plus QR 500 for housing and QR 300 for food unless the employer provides them <sup>[11](#o11), [9 (2020)](#o9)</sup>. Only the basic wage in the contract is owed; overtime, paid at basic plus 25%, is not guaranteed, and total work may not pass 10 hours a day <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2)</sup>. On QR 1,000 basic, two hours of overtime a day for 26 days adds about QR 300 <sup>[9 (2020)](#o9), [2 (2004)](#o2)</sup>. What Bangladeshis are reported to earn:
- Farm workers: about QR 1,000, sometimes with no food or housing allowance, often seven days a week without overtime pay (2025) <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>.
- Women housemaids: QR 1,000 to 1,200, with food and housing (2025) <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>.
- Audited World Cup sites (2022), the best case: average gross pay of QR 2,063 (about Tk 70,000) a month, overtime included <sup>[N43 (2022)](#n43)</sup>.

Overtime hours are often "forgotten" in pay, or part of the pay is given in cash outside the bank transfer <sup>[N43 (2022)](#n43)</sup>. The employer may not deduct for housing, may take at most 10% of the wage for a loan, and all deductions together may not pass 50% (see Deductions) <sup>[45 (2014)](#o45), [2 (2004)](#o2)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In 2026, what is a Bangladeshi labourer, cleaner, security guard, light driver, heavy driver and bus driver in Qatar paid a month, basic and with overtime?
> - How many overtime hours a month do most Bangladeshi workers get?
> - What does food cost a worker who gets the QR 300 allowance instead of meals?

<a id="housing-and-heat"></a>

#### 1.4.4 Housing and heat

The housing standards, the summer midday work ban and how to report a breach are in Work visa: company worker (see Housing; see Heat).

> **Need to know**
> - How many men share a room in a typical labour camp in 2026?
> - What time do the buses leave for work?

<a id="if-you-go-as-a-driver"></a>

#### 1.4.5 If you go as a driver

A driver visa does not come with a licence. You need <sup>[N44](#n44), [N45](#n45)</sup>:
- your RP and QID;
- a letter from your sponsor approving the licence;
- a course at an approved driving school.

What it costs:
- full course of about 40 classes: QR 3,300 to 4,000 (Tk 1.1 to 1.4 lakh) <sup>[N36](#n36), [N45](#n45)</sup>;
- 20-class half course, if you hold a Bangladesh smart-card licence: QR 2,400 to 2,800 <sup>[N36](#n36), [N45](#n45)</sup>;
- MOI learner's permit: QR 150 <sup>[N44](#n44)</sup>;
- MOI test: QR 50 per attempt <sup>[N44](#n44)</sup>;
- MOI licence: QR 250 <sup>[N44](#n44)</sup>.

A course gives two chances at the road test; after two failures the school may require another course <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>. The eye test can be done at QVC before you fly <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>. A licence from a GCC (Gulf Cooperation Council) country allows a direct test; a Bangladeshi licence is not on the short list that can be exchanged <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Who pays the driving course for a company driver or a house driver, the employer or the worker?
> - What happens to a worker who keeps failing the test?
> - How many months does a new house driver usually wait for his licence?
> - Can a Bangladeshi licence holder go straight to the test?
> - How many attempts do Bangladeshis usually need?

<a id="sending-money-home"></a>

#### 1.4.6 Sending money home

Legal channels include exchange houses and sending straight to a bKash wallet through partners such as Gulf Exchange and Doha Exchange (see [Sending money home](#sending-money-home)) <sup>[46](#o46), [N46](#n46)</sup>. Workers use hundi (unlicensed money transfer) because its operators deliver hand to hand, while legal channels mean travel and effort <sup>[N47](#n47)</sup>. MOI arrests people for unlicensed currency exchange <sup>[N48 (2024)](#n48)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many more taka per riyal does hundi give than a bank or bKash in 2026?
> - Do lenders and brokers in Bangladesh take repayment through the same hundi agent?
> - How much does a Bangladeshi worker in Qatar typically send home a month in 2026?

<a id="what-ties-you-to-the-employer"></a>

### 1.5 What ties you to the employer

Since 2020 the law ties you to the employer only to enter Qatar and to keep your residence permit (RP) alive: you can quit, change employer and leave Qatar without its permission <sup>[3 (2015)](#o3), [10 (2020)](#o10), [11](#o11)</sup>. In practice the employer, your sponsor or "kafeel", still holds your RP, often your passport, and the power to cancel the RP or report you as having run away, and most employers hiring inside Qatar still ask for the old employer's sign-off <sup>[N5 (2021)](#n5), [N6](#n6), [N7](#n7)</sup>.

- **Entering.** A work entry permit is granted only under a contract with the employer recruiting you, and a visa can't be moved, sold or traded <sup>[3 (2015)](#o3)</sup>. Before your RP is issued, the visa can move to another employer only with MOL's approval <sup>[14 (2019)](#o14)</sup>.
- **Renewing.** The employer alone must renew the RP, within 90 days of its expiry ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 7, item 2: Make sure the employer issues your residence permit (RP) and QID](#step-7-after-you-arrive-item-2)) <sup>[3 (2015)](#o3)</sup>. If it doesn't, you, not the employer, take the punishment <sup>[N5 (2021)](#n5)</sup>.
- **Quitting.** You give one month's written notice in your first two years and two months after that, or leave at once with full end-of-service gratuity if the employer breaks the contract or the law <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2)</sup>.
- **Changing employer.** No NOC (no-objection certificate) is needed; you notify MOL while your RP is valid or expired less than 90 days, and the new employer's registration must be active (see [If you want to change employer](#if-you-want-to-change-employer)) <sup>[11](#o11), [47 (2020)](#o47), [N49](#n49)</sup>. Most employers hiring inside Qatar still ask for a resignation letter signed by the old employer, which workers call the "release paper" <sup>[N7](#n7), [N50 (2023)](#n50), [N6](#n6)</sup>; some old employers charged QR 5,000 to 15,000 (Tk 1.7 to 5.1 lakh) for it, even after the contract had ended <sup>[N5 (2021)](#n5), [N6](#n6)</sup>. Before starting a change, check for an absconding report (see If your employer reports you absent; how to check is still a Need to know there) <sup>[N51 (2020)](#n51)</sup>.
- **Leaving Qatar.** There is no exit permit <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. What holds people back is a passport the employer keeps, dues they lose by leaving, and the ticket: the employer must pay the ticket home only at the end of service, and leaving without serving notice bars a new Qatar work permit for one year <sup>[N6](#n6), [2 (2004)](#o2)</sup>. Workers have been made to sign, by thumbprint, papers saying they received dues they were never paid <sup>[N52 (2022)](#n52)</sup>. Domestic workers must tell the employer 72 hours before leaving, which gives an abusive employer time to retaliate <sup>[11](#o11), [N6](#n6)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Must a new employer have an unused visa slot (quota) for the trade before MOL approves a transfer?
> - Who pays the ticket home for a worker who quits before the contract ends, with or without notice?

<a id="which-law-covers-you"></a>

#### 1.5.1 Which law covers you

Company workers come under the Labour Law and domestic workers (house drivers, maids, nannies, cooks, gardeners) under the Domestic Workers Law (Law 15 of 2017) <sup>[4](#o4), [15 (2017)](#o15)</sup>. The Labour Law doesn't cover workers of ministries and government bodies, the armed forces and police, people working at sea, QatarEnergy and other state petroleum companies, casual workers, or farm and grazing workers, except those at farms that process or market their produce or who run or repair farm machinery <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which rules on notice, job change and complaints apply to a Bangladeshi on a farm, at sea or employed by a government body?

<a id="your-passport"></a>

#### 1.5.2 Your passport

The employer must hand it back once the RP is done unless you ask in writing for it to be kept, on pain of a fine of up to QR 25,000 ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 7, item 3: Keep your passport](#step-7-after-you-arrive-item-3)) <sup>[3 (2015)](#o3)</sup>. Most migrant workers' passports are still reported to be held by their employers <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>. Among domestic workers, 87 of 105 women interviewed in 2020 had had their passports taken <sup>[N53 (2020)](#n53), [N5 (2021)](#n5)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do Qatar companies make new Bangladeshi workers sign the written request to keep their passport?
> - How does a worker get the passport back when the company refuses: which office, and does it work?

<a id="absconding-charges"></a>

#### 1.5.3 Absconding charges

Leaving your employer's work is still an offence, even when escaping abuse <sup>[3 (2015)](#o3), [N54](#n54)</sup>. The employer files a report free, online or at MOI, and you must contest it in person <sup>[48](#o48), [N6](#n6)</sup>; it leads to a QR 12,000 (about Tk 4.1 lakh) settlement or prosecution, and deportation if unpaid (see If your employer reports you absent, What it can lead to) <sup>[49 (2015)](#o49), [3 (2015)](#o3)</sup>. Employers have filed it, cancelled the RP, or used MOL's "Leave Country Notice" to cancel a worker's QID when the worker started a job change or claimed dues <sup>[N6](#n6), [N55 (2023)](#n55)</sup>.

<a id="an-expired-rp"></a>

#### 1.5.4 An expired RP

Late renewal costs QR 10 a day up to QR 6,000 ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 7, item 2: Make sure the employer issues your residence permit (RP) and QID](#step-7-after-you-arrive-item-2)) <sup>[49 (2015)](#o49)</sup>. More than 90 days after expiry you can no longer change employer and must leave Qatar; staying on is settled at QR 12,000 <sup>[47 (2020)](#o47), [3 (2015)](#o3), [49 (2015)](#o49)</sup>. Without a valid QID you can't get a health card <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>. Qatar's last grace period, three months from 9 February 2025, let people without valid papers leave (see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers)) <sup>[N56](#n56)</sup>.

<a id="the-free-visa"></a>

#### 1.5.5 The "free visa"

Sellers also call it a "freelance" visa <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>. The sponsor gives no job but stays your legal tie: people already in Qatar pay a sponsor QR 5,000 to 10,000 (Tk 1.7 to 3.4 lakh) for one, and sometimes QR 250 to 500 (Tk 8,500 to 17,000) a month to stay sponsored <sup>[N57](#n57), [N6](#n6)</sup>. You still need the sponsor to renew your QID, bring family or cancel the visa, and sellers often shut the company and can't be found when renewal is due ([Work visa: company worker](#work-visa-company-worker), [Step 1, item 5: If a relative or friend in Qatar sends you a visa](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-5), The trap) <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>. Working for anyone else is the same offence as absconding <sup>[3 (2015)](#o3), [49 (2015)](#o49), [N57](#n57)</sup>. Sellers also demand thousands of riyals to let you change sponsor <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does a free-visa sponsor charge for the yearly QID renewal in 2026?

<a id="what-goes-wrong-most-and-what-people-wrongly-believe"></a>

### 1.6 What goes wrong most, and what people wrongly believe

Unpaid or late wages and end-of-service gratuity are the most common complaints by far, and the free visa leaves many workers in Qatar jobless, in debt and without papers <sup>[N58 (2022)](#n58), [N7](#n7), [N8](#n8)</sup>. Most complaints that are filed are settled or won, but winning is not the same as being paid <sup>[N58 (2022)](#n58), [N50 (2023)](#n50)</sup>.

<a id="what-workers-complain-about"></a>

#### 1.6.1 What workers complain about

MOL receives about 30,000 worker complaints a year, most about unpaid wages <sup>[N58 (2022)](#n58), [N7](#n7)</sup>. The patterns behind them:
- Labour-supply companies hold back wages until the client company pays them <sup>[N26](#n26)</sup>.
- Construction companies send workers home before the contract ends, on "long leave", without their dues <sup>[N52 (2022)](#n52)</sup>.
- Workers who came on traded visas and found no job go months without salary (2026) <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>.

Most deaths of migrant workers are recorded as "natural causes" and not meaningfully investigated, which denies families compensation (for the figures for Bangladeshis, see [If a worker dies](#if-a-worker-dies)) <sup>[N54](#n54), [N59](#n59)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What share of MOL's complaints come from Bangladeshis, and of what type?

<a id="how-few-complain"></a>

#### 1.6.2 How few complain

In a 2021 study by RMMRU (Refugee and Migratory Movements Research Unit) of 1,160 workers back from six Gulf states, Qatar among them, 92% never complained in the destination country: they didn't know where to, didn't believe the money could be recovered, or feared retaliation and blacklisting; returnees from Qatar had lost USD 2,075 each on average <sup>[N60 (2021)](#n60)</sup>.

<a id="a-free-visa-lets-you-work-for-anyone-you-like"></a>

#### 1.6.3 "A free visa lets you work for anyone you like."

Brokers say that once in Qatar you'll easily find work <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>. There is no free-visa category in Qatar; buying, selling and working on one are all illegal, and a free-visa worker who isn't paid has no legal recourse <sup>[N20](#n20), [N21](#n21), [51 (2021)](#o51), [N6](#n6)</sup>. Of free-visa workers in OKUP's five-country Gulf study, 21% got a work permit only after paying about Tk 1.5 lakh more and 14% could neither pay for one nor go home and stayed on illegally <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. In Qatar the ones without work look for illegal day labour and risk arrest and deportation; one who paid about Tk 7 lakh in 2016 was deported in 2020 with 25 riyals <sup>[N8](#n8), [N61 (2022)](#n61)</sup>. How to check a job before paying is in Work visa: company worker ([Step 1, item 4: Check the job, the demand letter and the employer](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4)).

<a id="go-on-a-visit-or-hayya-visa-and-change-it-to-a-work-visa-there"></a>

#### 1.6.4 "Go on a visit or Hayya visa and change it to a work visa there."

A visitor on a Hayya e-visa (Qatar's tourist e-visa) may not work, and the platform's chief executive said in 2023 that it cannot be converted into a work visa <sup>[52](#o52), [N62 (2023)](#n62)</sup>. Whether any visit visa can be changed inside Qatar: see [Changing visa type](#changing-visa-type).

<a id="since-2020-the-kafala-is-gone-so-you-can-walk-out-and-find-a-better-job"></a>

#### 1.6.5 "Since 2020 the kafala is gone, so you can walk out and find a better job."

The kafala (sponsorship) law changed, but not the employer's hold (see [What ties you to the employer](#what-ties-you-to-the-employer)): workers are still asked for a signed resignation letter, and Amnesty said in 2025 that employers still hold excessive power over workers' entry, residence and job changes <sup>[N54](#n54), [N59](#n59)</sup>. Walking out without the MOL transfer is "absconding" <sup>[3 (2015)](#o3)</sup>. For the legal route, see [If you want to change employer](#if-you-want-to-change-employer).

<a id="complaining-is-useless-it-works-on-paper-collecting-is-hard"></a>

#### 1.6.6 "Complaining is useless": it works on paper, collecting is hard

From October 2021 to October 2022, 66.5% of complaints to MOL were settled before or during conciliation, and the labour courts decided for the worker in 84% of the cases referred to them <sup>[N58 (2022)](#n58)</sup>. But companies often don't pay even after a court rules for them <sup>[N50 (2023)](#n50)</sup>, cases often take several months and up to a year <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>, and the Workers' Support and Insurance Fund pays only cases decided by a committee, about a quarter of them <sup>[N63](#n63), [53 (2018)](#o53)</sup>. For how to complain, see If you aren't paid, are abused or your passport is taken, item 2 <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does a worker live on during a wage case: does MOL, the Embassy or a charity give food or shelter?
> - Does the Fund pay anything before the decision?

<a id="how-the-irregular-routes-end"></a>

#### 1.6.7 How the irregular routes end
- *Running away or overstaying.* Both are settled at QR 12,000 or prosecuted, with deportation if the settlement isn't paid (see [What ties you to the employer](#what-ties-you-to-the-employer); for the way out, see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers)) <sup>[49 (2015)](#o49), [3 (2015)](#o3)</sup>.
- *Protesting unpaid wages.* In August 2022, after workers of the Al Bandary company, some unpaid for seven months, protested in Doha, police arrested protesters and workers from Bangladesh and four other countries were deported <sup>[N64 (2022)](#n64)</sup>. Workers are sometimes deported before their labour case is resolved <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are workers deported from Qatar held by police on arrival in Dhaka in 2026?
> - Can someone who left under the 2025 grace period come back on a new work visa?

<a id="visas-not-open-to-bangladeshis"></a>

## 2 Visas not open to Bangladeshis

These schemes exist but are closed to Bangladesh passport holders, or open only in narrow cases <sup>[1](#o1), [54](#o54)</sup>.

- **Visa-free entry or visa on arrival, including MOI's free 90-day and free 30-day (extendable) visas on arrival:** Bangladesh is in no visa-free or visa-on-arrival group, nor among the 43 and 39 countries on those lists; a Bangladeshi applies for the Hayya A1 tourist e-visa (Hayya is Qatar's e-visa platform) <sup>[1](#o1), [54](#o54), [55](#o55), [56](#o56)</sup>.
- **Free 30-day visa on arrival with a hotel booked through Discover Qatar** (discoverqatar.qa, Qatar Airways' tour company and the only hotel site MOI accepts for it): only Pakistani, Indian and Thai nationals can get it <sup>[57](#o57), [58](#o58)</sup>.
- **The visa-on-arrival groups on Visit Qatar's nationality checker** (visitqatar.com, Qatar's official tourism site), with a QR 100 fee, a Discover Qatar hotel, or both: only other named nationalities can get them (such as Mauritius, Taiwan, Iran, Pakistan, Thailand and Ukraine) <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Qatar-Oman Joint Tourist Visa on arrival (30 days):** Bangladesh is not on the list <sup>[59](#o59), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Special on-arrival tourist and business visas:** only nationals of Turkey, Belarus, Iran, Poland and Hungary can get them <sup>[60](#o60)</sup>.
- **72-hour business visa on arrival (QR 50):** only other nationalities can get it <sup>[61](#o61), [60](#o60)</sup>.
- **Hayya F1 (visa-free, 2-year multiple entry):** only US citizens can get it <sup>[52](#o52), [62](#o62)</sup>.
- **Visa-free entry for GCC citizens:** only GCC nationals can enter this way <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Hayya Official Visa and Hayya Business:** these are issued only on invitation by government or semi-government bodies <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.
- **Hayya Transit Visa (4 days, single entry):** it is issued only through the aviation industry, for transit passengers <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.
- **Entry visa for truck drivers resident in a GCC state:** it is only for those drivers and their helpers, at land entry points <sup>[14 (2019)](#o14)</sup>.
- **Return visa for domestic workers of Qatari diplomats:** it is only for domestic workers in the homes of Qatari diplomats posted abroad <sup>[14 (2019)](#o14)</sup>.

<a id="work-visa-company-worker"></a>

## 3 Work visa: company worker

The employer gets a recruitment approval from MOL and the work visa on company sponsorship (QR 300) from MOI in Qatar; in Bangladesh the worker gets his papers ready, gives his fingerprints, signs the contract (where it applies) and takes the medical at QVC, then gets emigration clearance from BMET and flies; in Qatar the employer completes his residence permit <sup>[8](#o8), [7 (2023)](#o7), [35](#o35), [36](#o36), [17](#o17), [38 (2021)](#o38)</sup>. On the official route a licensed Bangladeshi agency selects the worker from BMET's database <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>; an employer can also recruit for its own account with MOL's approval <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2)</sup>; a worker who got his visa through his own efforts, not through an agency, applies for BMET clearance himself ([Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance, insurance, a loan and the flight](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight)) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="who-pays-what"></a>

### 3.1 Who pays what

<a id="before-paying-what-the-law-allows"></a>

#### 3.1.1 Before paying: what the law allows

In Qatar a licensed recruiter may not charge the worker any fees, recruitment expenses or other costs <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2)</sup>; breaking this carries up to 1 month's prison and/or a fine of QR 2,000 to 6,000, and the court may close the office and revoke its licence <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2)</sup>. Since June 2026 a recruiter that repeats the breach can be named on MOL's website, and the Minister can fine recruitment violations <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>. In BMET's posted letter for BTS Overseas (RL2434), for women housemaids, the employer bears the migration cost and all costs up to arrival in Qatar, such as the airfare <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does any Qatari rule stop the employer itself from recovering visa or recruitment costs from the worker, given that the ban covers licensed recruiters <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2)</sup>?

<a id="the-bangladesh-maximum"></a>

#### 3.1.2 The Bangladesh maximum

The government may fix by order the most a worker pays to go abroad <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>. News reports give Tk 1,00,780 for Qatar, set in 2018 <sup>[N37 (2018)](#n37), [N47](#n47)</sup>, and a district employment officer said in 2025 that the maximum for six Gulf countries including Qatar is Tk 1.15 to 1.2 lakh <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the current government maximum for Qatar: is the 2018 figure of Tk 1,00,780 still in force, or has a newer order replaced it?

<a id="how-to-pay"></a>

#### 3.1.3 How to pay

Pay the agency only through the bank, into the agency's own bank account, keep every receipt, and don't go to middlemen <sup>[44](#o44), [17](#o17)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-find-a-real-job"></a>

### 3.2 Step 1: Find a real job

1. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-1"></a>**Register on OEP.** Agencies must select workers from BMET's database, and BMET gives no clearance to anyone outside it <sup>[32](#o32), [30](#o30)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>:
   - your NID (national ID card);
   - a mobile phone that can receive codes;
   - a bKash, Nagad or other mobile-banking account to pay.

   1. In Google Chrome open employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/login-with-otp (or scan the QR code on BMET's guide) and press "অনুগ্রহ করে এখানে ক্লিক করুন" (Please click here) twice <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
   2. Type every detail exactly as on your NID, with no dots in the name and spaces where the NID has them, and press "যাচাই করুন" (Verify) <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
   3. Type the OTP (one-time code) sent to your mobile and press Submit, then "সংরক্ষণ করুন ও এগিয়ে যান" (Save and continue), and fill in the full form correctly <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
   4. Press "পেমেন্ট করতে এগিয়ে যান" (Proceed to payment), then "পেমেন্ট করুন" (Pay); choose Mobile Banking, then bKash, Nagad or another service, and enter the account number, OTP and PIN <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
   5. Download your Registration Card; your BMET registration is then complete <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What is the OEP registration fee in 2026 <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>?

   **If it fails:** call the OEP hotline, 09614445271 <sup>[64](#o64)</sup>, or Probash-Bondhu, the 24-hour call centre of the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (WEWB, the Ministry's welfare body for migrant workers and their families): 16135 free in Bangladesh, +8809610102030 from abroad <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-2"></a>**Learn a trade if you need one.** On 18 May 2026 Qatar's Labour Minister said Qatar wants workers from 5 specific TTCs in four trades: electrician, plumber, air-conditioning (AC) technician and welding <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>. In Qatar some professions may be done only with a training and test certificate from a centre accredited by MOL, which publishes the list of professions on its website <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>. Food handlers can also take the Food Handler Licensing Examination of Qatar's Ministry of Public Health (MoPH) before travel, at accredited centres in several countries listed on MoPH's website; it is optional and not needed for recruitment, and a pass means no re-test in Qatar for the professional practice licence <sup>[N66](#n66)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which five TTCs did Qatar's minister name?
   > - How does a worker apply for these jobs?
   > - Which professions are on MOL's list?
   > - Is any MOL or MoPH accredited centre in Bangladesh?

   You'll need: your OEP registration, and a current photo in your OEP profile that matches your face today <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.

   1. Log in on OEP with the code sent to your mobile, open Training portal from your profile, then Training, then Trade/Skills; choose your division, district and training centre and press Search <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
   2. Pick the trade and press Apply; check your photo, press Continue, then Process to Enroll and Confirm; "My Course" lists your applications <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>.
   3. Pay the fee BMET sets for short courses against a money receipt; courses run by development partners or projects are free and pay a monthly stipend; courses last from 3 days to 4 years <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

   **Scams:** if anyone offers to get you into or through a TTC, or to get your clearance, for money, tell the nearest law-enforcement office <sup>[44](#o44)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3"></a>**Check the agency's licence.** OEP's agency list shows each licensed agency's RL number, name, address, office phone, licence status and validity date <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.

   You'll need: the agency's name and RL number <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.

   1. Open www.oep.gov.bd/agencies (free) and find the row with the RL number; a real row reads, for example, "RL2434, BTS OVERSEAS LIMITED, 31/C-1, THPKHANA ROAD, Ramna, Dhaka, 01850907993, Active, 02/May/2029" <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>.
   2. Read the License Status: Active, Expired, Cancelled, Suspended or Server Locked <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>. A licence can show "Cancelled" while its validity date is years ahead: RL0345 shows "Cancelled" with a validity date of 31 December 2028 <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>. An agency that recruits after its licence is cancelled commits an offence carrying 1 to 2 years' prison and a fine of Tk 1 to 5 lakh <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - What does "Server Locked" mean on OEP's agency list <sup>[66](#o66)</sup>?

   **If a broker brings you the job.** An agency may appoint only a registered person as its sub-agent <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. Acting as a sub-agent without registration carries 1 year's prison or a fine of Tk 50,000 to 2 lakh, and an agency that appoints one in breach of the rule 1 year or Tk 1 to 5 lakh <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Is there a public list of registered sub-agents a worker can check a broker's name against, given that the law leaves registration to rules <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>?

   **Which agencies hold Qatar job orders.** BMET posted permission letters at bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos from June 2023 until it stopped on 10 December 2025 <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>. "Posts" means the number of workers a letter lets the agency process, not the number who went <sup>[67](#o67)</sup>. For Qatar there are eight letters from seven agencies, 2,261 posts in all, about 2% of the 107,598 workers BMET cleared for Qatar in 2025 alone, so they show who holds posted Qatar job orders, not who sends the most people <sup>[68 (2024)](#o68), [69 (2024)](#o69), [70 (2024)](#o70), [71 (2024)](#o71), [72](#o72), [73](#o73), [74](#o74), [32](#o32), [6](#o6)</sup>. Most letters are titled only by memo and RL number, with no country, so searching কাতার on the list finds just one of the eight <sup>[75](#o75), [18](#o18)</sup>.

   The letters show what the agency may take, and it varies for the same job: three agencies recruiting 6G welders and helpers for the same employer, Montiara Contracting and Trading Company, may take Tk 92,000, Tk 92,000 and Tk 70,000 a worker, by cheque, bank draft or pay order only <sup>[68 (2024)](#o68), [69 (2024)](#o69), [71 (2024)](#o71)</sup>. On the women's letters the employer bears the whole migration cost, so the agency may take nothing <sup>[72](#o72), [73](#o73)</sup>.

   The agencies, by posts:

   - **Barcelona Overseas (RL1380):** 1,000 posts (30 Jun 2025): 700 housemaids at QR 1,100 a month, 150 babysitters and 150 cooks at QR 1,200, all women, for Al-Fursan Manpower Recruitment, Doha <sup>[72](#o72)</sup>. In Nov 2019 the Bangladesh embassy in Riyadh named a "Barcelona Overseas", with no RL number given, among agencies that sent women to Saudi Arabia with their age raised <sup>[N67 (2019)](#n67)</sup>.
   - **T.M. Overseas (RL0498):** 700 posts (4 Sep 2025): 500 housemaids at QR 1,200 to 1,500, 100 babysitters and 100 cooks at QR 1,300, all women, for SAMA ALKHAIR MANPOWER, Doha <sup>[73](#o73)</sup>. On Recruitment Advisor, the worker-review site of the ITUC (International Trade Union Confederation), a Feb 2023 review says it gave a false contract for Bahrain and took Tk 3.5 lakh, and another that a Tk 8 lakh Saudi job paid less than promised and ended in a re-entry ban <sup>[N68](#n68)</sup>.
   - **Bonanza Overseas (RL0579):** 247 posts in two letters for Urban Hospitality Services, Doha: 210 cleaners, cooks, waiters, technicians and drivers at QR 1,000 to 2,000 with a cap of Tk 19,560 (Nov 2024), and 37 mostly operating-theatre technicians (May 2025) <sup>[70 (2024)](#o70), [74](#o74)</sup>. Outside evidence is later in this item ([Step 1, item 3: Check the agency's licence](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3)).
   - **BTS Overseas (RL2434):** 100 posts (5 Aug 2025): women housemaids for OKAZ Manpower, Al Messila <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>.
   - **Ar-Rafi Overseas (RL1790):** 90 posts (9 Sep 2024): 30 welders at QR 1,980 to 2,100 and 60 helpers at QR 1,000 for Montiara (spelt "Montira" on this letter), cap Tk 92,000 <sup>[68 (2024)](#o68)</sup>.
   - **Stanford Employment (RL1352):** 84 posts (9 Sep 2024) for Montiara, cap Tk 92,000 <sup>[69 (2024)](#o69)</sup>. A Ministry gazette revoked its licence in July 2026 among 49 agencies in the Malaysia syndicate case, the Appellate Division let the revocation stand in October 2026, and OEP shows it "Cancelled" <sup>[N69](#n69), [N70](#n70), [66](#o66)</sup>.
   - **Zirabo Overseas (RL1458):** 40 posts (Nov 2024): 30 welders and 10 helpers for Montiara, Tk 70,000 <sup>[71 (2024)](#o71)</sup>. Bonik Barta (Oct 2025) reported its licence among 45 cancelled for not sending even 100 workers, yet OEP still shows it "Active" <sup>[N71](#n71), [66](#o66)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Is RL1380 the "Barcelona Overseas" the Riyadh embassy named in Nov 2019?
   > - Is Zirabo Overseas (RL1458) licensed to recruit today, or was its licence cancelled on 30 Oct 2025?

   **The one agency with outside evidence of fair recruitment.** Bonanza Overseas was the Bangladeshi agency in the 2018-21 ILO and MOL pilot, recruiting for STS, the company placing workers on QDVC construction sites (see [How a job reaches you](#how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut), and who takes a cut) <sup>[N32 (2022)](#n32)</sup>. It advertised jobs in local media and recruited directly in workers' villages to cut out fee-charging brokers <sup>[N72](#n72)</sup>. Workers' average cost fell from USD 3,408 before STS changed its policy to USD 300 after the pilot, mostly their own passport and travel inside Bangladesh <sup>[N72](#n72)</sup>. The evidence covers that one construction channel; its Doha hospitality cap of Tk 19,560 compares with Tk 70,000 to 92,000 on the Montiara letters <sup>[N32 (2022)](#n32), [70 (2024)](#o70), [68 (2024)](#o68), [71 (2024)](#o71)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What did Bonanza's 2024-25 Urban Hospitality recruits actually pay, including any broker's cut?

   Every agency's licence, letters for other countries and cited flags are in the shared Recruiting agencies list (`agencies/README.md`).

4. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4"></a>**Check the job, the demand letter and the employer.** A real Qatar job starts with the employer: MOL approves the recruitment, MOI issues the visa, and the Bangladesh Embassy in Doha attests the demand letter <sup>[7 (2023)](#o7), [31 (2021)](#o31)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2), [31 (2021)](#o31), [7 (2023)](#o7)</sup>:
   - the job, wage, contract length and employer's name in writing;
   - the demand letter;
   - the Qatar visa approval letter;
   - the employer's establishment ID (its registration number with the Qatar authorities).

   1. Check the written terms against what a Qatar contract must contain and the legal minimum wage, hours, overtime, leave and probation (see [Your rights at work](#your-rights-at-work)) <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2), [9 (2020)](#o9)</sup>.
   2. Ask the agency for the demand letter attested by the Bangladesh Embassy in Doha, and the Qatar government's visa approval letter submitted with it; the employer, its PRO (the company's government-relations clerk) or its agent files them at the Embassy with its agreement with the agent and the company's details <sup>[31 (2021)](#o31)</sup>. The Embassy checks the employer and attests the demand and visa before the agency selects workers <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Can a worker check with the Embassy the reference number printed beside the employer on a BMET permission letter (for example "LW-QA-456, 03/06/2025") <sup>[32](#o32), [31 (2021)](#o31)</sup>?

   3. Look for BMET's permission letter for the job order: open bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos and search কাতার (Qatar in Bangla; the English word finds nothing) <sup>[18](#o18), [76](#o76)</sup>. A real letter shows the memo number and date, the agency and its RL number, the Qatar employer and its address, the reference number beside the employer, the job, the number of workers, the salary, the contract terms and who bears the migration cost <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>. It also requires the agency to file an undertaking, on a Tk 300 non-judicial stamp, that the contract terms will be kept, the workers will get the job and the pay, housing and benefits offered, and that if anything goes wrong the agency will pay the worker's food, lodging and return costs and any compensation the authorities impose <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>. The search finds only one of the eight Qatar letters BMET posted; all eight are listed in [Step 1, item 3: Check the agency's licence](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3) (Which agencies hold Qatar job orders) <sup>[67](#o67), [75](#o75), [18](#o18)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Does BMET still issue permission letters for Qatar jobs since it stopped posting them?
      > - Can a worker get a copy of a permission letter from BMET?

   4. Ask for the employer's establishment ID: MOL approves recruitment only for a company whose ID is active and not banned <sup>[7 (2023)](#o7)</sup>. Qatar's Ministry of Commerce and Industry promotes a Company Inquiry Service, ucs.moci.gov.qa, to review a company's record before dealing with it <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Does the Company Inquiry Service open free without a Qatari login (its page wouldn't load for us) <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>?
      > - What does the Company Inquiry Service show about a company <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>?

   **What happens in practice:** workers report paying agencies Tk 3 to 4.2 lakh for Qatar construction, garment and electrician jobs; three got a fake contract letter, two of them didn't get the promised job or pay, and the electrician got the job and pay promised <sup>[N74 (2023)](#n74), [N75 (2023)](#n75), [N76 (2023)](#n76)</sup>.

5. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-5"></a>**If a relative or friend in Qatar sends you a visa.** The relative arranges the visa in Qatar, often after paying the employer or someone else, and a Dhaka broker or agency does only the medical, the BMET card and the ticket <sup>[N77 (2017)](#n77), [N78 (2017)](#n78)</sup>. BMET calls a visa cleared one at a time a "single" visa, whoever found it; it charges source tax of Tk 250 if the Embassy in Doha attested the contract and Tk 400 if not <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>.

   **What the relative may be sending.** All five arrive looking like a real Qatar visa, and a bought visa also shows as genuine on MOI's checker <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>:
   - *A real job with the relative's own employer.* The employer gets MOL's approval and pays MOI's QR 300 visa fee ([Step 3, item 2: The employer gets the visa from MOI](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-visa-item-2)) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Processors call it a "company visa": you work at the company and in the job written on the visa <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>.
   - *A bought ("free") visa.* A sponsor or company with spare visas sells them on through several hands, and the worker must find his own work <sup>[N78 (2017)](#n78), [N34](#n34)</sup>. Working for anyone but the sponsor is illegal in Qatar, and buying or selling a visa is a crime in Bangladesh too (see [How a job reaches you](#how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut), and who takes a cut) <sup>[3 (2015)](#o3), [28](#o28)</sup>.
   - *A labour-supply (manpower) company visa.* A genuine company visa, but the company hires workers out to client sites, recruiters often let workers believe they are direct hires, and wages are often "late until the client pays" <sup>[N26](#n26)</sup>. **Sign:** the company on the visa is not the company at the site where you'll work.
   - *A household visa, often a "house-driver visa".* The sponsor is a Qatari individual, not a company, and you come under the Domestic Workers Law, not the Labour Law (see [Work visa: domestic worker](#work-visa-domestic-worker)) <sup>[N34](#n34), [15 (2017)](#o15), [4](#o4)</sup>. If the sponsor on the visa is a person and you were promised company work, the visa is not what you were told; a driving job promised by a relative and a building site on arrival is a known switch (a 2016 Noakhali case: see [What goes wrong most](#what-goes-wrong-most-and-what-people-wrongly-believe), later in this item) <sup>[N79 (2016)](#n79)</sup>. What a Qatar driving licence takes and costs is in Background (see What you pay, and what you earn and keep, If you go as a driver).
   - *A family visit or Hayya visa sold as a way into work.* A Hayya visa never allows work <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>, and a visit visa can't be turned into a work visa inside Qatar: you must leave and re-enter (see [Changing visa type](#changing-visa-type)) <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>. A family visit visa needs a host who is a first-degree relative and has family accommodation in his own name <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which words do Dhaka processors and relatives use for a company visa, a bought visa and a household visa, beyond "company visa", "free visa" and "house-driver visa", which we found in print <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>?
   > - Are "azad visa" and "attested / non-attested visa" also used?

   **Cost.** Free-visa workers spent an average of Tk 4,99,802 in 2025, against a government-set average of Tk 1,16,224 <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>; in May 2026 the reported range was Tk 4 to 7 lakh <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>. What people already in Qatar pay a sponsor for one is in Background (see [What ties you to the employer](#what-ties-you-to-the-employer)). 21% of free-visa workers paid about Tk 1.5 lakh more after arrival to get their work permit (iqama), and 14% never got one and stayed on illegally <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.
   - **Paid by the employer:** the MOI visa (QR 300) and the QR 490 contract and medical fee at QVC ([Step 3, item 3: The employer puts the contract into MOI's system](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-visa-item-3)) <sup>[8](#o8), [79 (2021)](#o79)</sup>. QVC said in 2025 that appointments are free and nobody needs to be paid for one <sup>[N30](#n30)</sup>, so any QVC charge passed on to you is a markup.
   - **Paid by you in Bangladesh:** the passport (Tk 4,025 regular, 5 years) <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>, the police clearance ([Step 2, item 2: Get a police clearance certificate (PCC)](#step-2-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-2)) <sup>[81](#o81)</sup>, the PDO (pre-departure orientation) course fee <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>, and BMET's fees ([Step 1, item 5: If a relative or friend in Qatar sends you a visa](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-5), sub-step 5: PDO and BMET clearance) <sup>[82](#o82), [44](#o44), [17](#o17)</sup>.
   - **The ticket:** in BMET's posted letter for BTS Overseas (RL2434), for women housemaids, the employer pays the airfare <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>; on this route the worker usually buys it through the Dhaka broker <sup>[N78 (2017)](#n78)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - In 2026, what does a relative in Doha charge for a visa from his own company when the company charged nothing?
   > - In 2026, what does a broker charge for the same visa?
   > - Under Qatari law, does the employer have to pay a company worker's ticket, given that Qatar's fee ban covers licensed recruiters only <sup>[2 (2004)](#o2)</sup>?

   **You'll need from the relative, before any money moves:**
   - a clear copy of the visa printout, with the visa number, your passport number, the sponsor's name and whether it is a company or a person, the job title and the wage <sup>[83](#o83), [N80](#n80), [N34](#n34)</sup>;
   - the MOI reference (visa) number the employer uses to book QVC <sup>[N4 (2018)](#n4), [N30](#n30)</sup>;
   - a photo of the company's establishment card (its registration with the Qatar authorities) <sup>[7 (2023)](#o7)</sup>;
   - the company's name in Arabic and English and where the work site is;
   - the phone number of another Bangladeshi working there now.

   Give him a passport valid for more than 195 days before he applies ([Step 2, item 1: Get a passport valid for more than 195 days](#step-2-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-1)) <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>. The visa must be used within 90 days of issue ([Step 3, item 5: The visa must be used in time](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-visa-item-5)) <sup>[14 (2019)](#o14)</sup>; "Qatar visas are closing, pay now" is an old broker tactic <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does a Qatar work visa stay valid if the worker renews his passport after it is issued, or must the sponsor apply again?

   1. **Check the visa, the sponsor and the job.** Have BMET check the visa first ([Step 5, item 2: At BMET](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself-item-2)) <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>, then MOI's Visa Inquiry ([Step 5, item 1: On MOI's Visa Inquiry](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself-item-1)) <sup>[85 (2022)](#o85)</sup>. Since a bought visa also shows as genuine, check what no checker can <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>: whether the company on the visa is the one you'll work for, and whether the job title matches the job promised <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>. Call the Bangladeshi working there now.

   2. **Check the wage in writing.** The minimum wage is QR 1,000 a month basic, plus QR 500 for housing and QR 300 for food unless the employer provides them, and a lower wage is void ([Your rights at work](#your-rights-at-work)) <sup>[11](#o11), [86 (2020)](#o86)</sup>. If the visa or contract shows less than QR 1,000 basic, or neither housing and food nor the QR 800 for them, don't pay.

   3. **At QVC, read the contract and wait for the medical.** The employer or you book on qatarvisacenter.com with the visa reference number (VRN) ([Step 4, item 1: Book the appointment](#step-4-qatar-visa-center-contract-fingerprints-and-medical-item-1)) <sup>[N4 (2018)](#n4), [N30](#n30)</sup>; pay no one for the booking <sup>[N30](#n30)</sup>; fake agents are covered in [Step 4: Qatar Visa Center: contract, fingerprints and medical](#step-4-qatar-visa-center-contract-fingerprints-and-medical) (see [Fake QVC agents](#fake-qvc-agents)) <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>. At the biometric booth read the job and wage before you sign the contract <sup>[36](#o36)</sup>. The result goes to the employer; QVC's tracker shows "Medical Test Complete" and then "Results Available with Employer" <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>. If you are found unfit, the visa is cancelled, and one worker was told to pay the new visa fee himself <sup>[N31 (2022)](#n31)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - When a worker is found unfit at QVC, does a relative who sold him the visa ever return the money?

   4. **Ask whether the contract is attested.** For an individual male worker, the sponsor or its PRO has it attested at the Bangladesh Embassy in Doha ([Step 3, item 4: The Bangladesh Embassy in Doha attests the papers](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-work-visa-item-4)) <sup>[88 (2021)](#o88)</sup>. Dhaka processors say clearance on a non-attested Qatar visa needs extra checks and costs more <sup>[N81](#n81)</sup>. In June 2026 sources alleged that agencies and brokers get BMET clearance on unattested visas, charging Tk 4 to 6 lakh, and that many such workers find no company on arrival <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - In 2026, does BMET's emigration branch refuse a Qatar single visa whose contract the Embassy in Doha hasn't attested?
      > - Which agencies processed the unattested Qatar visas reported in June 2026?

   5. **PDO and BMET clearance (the "manpower card").** Do the PDO first ([Step 6, item 1: Do the compulsory 3-day pre-departure orientation (PDO)](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight-item-1)), then apply for clearance yourself with the papers in [Step 6, item 3: Get BMET clearance (the smart card)](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight-item-3); it takes 3 working days <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. A Dhaka processor says a worker who got his own visa can also apply on BMET's online platform, oc.bmet.gov.bd <sup>[N81](#n81)</sup>. The official fees are in [Step 6, item 3: Get BMET clearance (the smart card)](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-insurance-a-loan-and-the-flight-item-3) <sup>[82](#o82), [17](#o17)</sup>. One Dhaka processor charges Tk 9,000 all-in on an attested visa and Tk 9,500 to 10,000 on a non-attested one <sup>[N81](#n81)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Who attests the copy of the OEP registration card for a worker clearing his own visa: a gazetted officer, or BMET itself?

   6. **Pay in stages, against proof.** The proof points are, in order: a BMET and MOI check plus a written wage at or above the legal minimum (sub-steps 1 and 2); "Medical Test Complete" on QVC's tracker (sub-step 3); and, last, a QID (Qatar ID card) issued in your name by the same company (sub-step 7) <sup>[17](#o17), [11](#o11), [87](#o87), [38 (2021)](#o38)</sup>. The QID is the real test, because the extra payment for a work permit comes after arrival (see Cost, earlier in this item) <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>. BMET tells workers to pay through the bank and keep receipts <sup>[44](#o44)</sup>; money for traded visas usually moves in cash or by hundi <sup>[N2 (2022)](#n2)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Do families take a stamp-paper deed, a security cheque or a witnessed receipt from a relative selling a Qatar visa, and has any of them helped recover money?

   7. **After you land: get the QID, then check the first pay.** The employer applies for your residence permit (RP, carried on the QID) after your medical and fingerprints in Qatar; tracking it, and what to do if it isn't issued or you can't find the company, are in [Step 7: After you arrive](#step-7-after-you-arrive) <sup>[38 (2021)](#o38), [89 (2021)](#o89), [51 (2021)](#o51)</sup>. Your wage must reach a Qatar bank account through WPS (the Wage Protection System) by the dates in [Step 7, item 7: Get your wages into a bank account](#step-7-after-you-arrive-item-7), and be at least the minimum (sub-step 2) <sup>[90](#o90), [11](#o11)</sup>. If it doesn't, you can ask MOL to change employer or end the contract, and the employer must pay your ticket home (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid-are-abused-or-your-passport-is-taken), are abused or your passport is taken, item 6) <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>.

   8. **If there is no job, or the wrong job.** You don't need the sponsor's permission (NOC, no-objection certificate) to move <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>: get the QID first, then apply (see [If you want to change employer](#if-you-want-to-change-employer)); before the RP is issued, the authorities can move your work visa to another employer with MOL's approval <sup>[47 (2020)](#o47), [14 (2019)](#o14)</sup>. Act before any absconding report is filed: a labour ministry official said in 2020 that a report filed first blocks the change <sup>[N51 (2020)](#n51)</sup>. In 2023 employers still demanded a signed resignation letter, which workers call "release papers" <sup>[N50 (2023)](#n50)</sup>; Bangla guides call changing employer "kafala change" <sup>[N45](#n45)</sup>.

   **The trap: paying for what the sponsor must do free.** The sponsor alone must issue and renew your RP <sup>[3 (2015)](#o3)</sup>. In 2020 a Bangladeshi labourer was asked for QR 4,000 to issue his RP and threatened with a runaway case if he didn't pay <sup>[N82 (2020)](#n82)</sup>. In 2024 a buyer paid QR 10,000 for a visa with no job, then QR 3,000 to renew his QID, still not renewed 2.5 months later <sup>[N57](#n57)</sup>. Some employers still demand fees to "release" workers, though the law no longer needs it <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>. Working for a company other than your sponsor is settled at QR 12,000 or prosecuted, with deportation if unpaid (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported); for an absconding case, see [If your employer reports you absent](#if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-absconding)) <sup>[49 (2015)](#o49), [3 (2015)](#o3), [51 (2021)](#o51)</sup>. MOI's last grace period for people without valid papers ran from 9 February 2025 for three months (see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers)) <sup>[N56](#n56)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Have Qatar's residency raids continued in 2025-26, after the latest report we found, from 2019 <sup>[N83 (2019)](#n83)</sup>?
   > - Do the raids still pick up people whose ID expired more than three months earlier and people working for another company, as in 2019 <sup>[N83 (2019)](#n83)</sup>?

   **What goes wrong most.**
   - *No company, or no job.* In 2023 workers who paid Tk 4 to 5 lakh waited months; one who came through an acquaintance waited 3 months and 10 days in Doha, then was given QR 1,000 and told nothing more would come <sup>[N84 (2023)](#n84)</sup>. Free-visa workers then look for illegal day labour and face arrest or deportation <sup>[N8](#n8)</sup>.
   - *A different job or a lower wage.* In 2016 a Noakhali man borrowed Tk 3.25 lakh at 30% a year after a relative in Qatar promised QR 2,000 a month as a driver; he was put on a building site on QR 600 basic plus QR 200 for food <sup>[N79 (2016)](#n79)</sup>. In 2017 a worker sent a visa by his maternal uncle learned on arrival he was down as a carpenter and was paid QR 700, not the QR 950 promised <sup>[N78 (2017)](#n78)</sup>. Both wages are below today's legal minimum (sub-step 2) <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.
   - *A runaway case.* Employers have filed false absconding reports against workers who complained to MOL (see [If your employer reports you absent](#if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-absconding)) <sup>[N82 (2020)](#n82), [51 (2021)](#o51)</sup>.
   - *Debt.* Most free-visa migrants borrowed, and those who mortgaged land and came back without work couldn't recover it <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.

   **If the relative cheats you.**
   1. *Settlement.* In villages, brokers who fail to send a client have been pushed through a shalish (village elders' arbitration) to repay, in one 2017 case half the money <sup>[N78 (2017)](#n78)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Is a written settlement signed before witnesses or at the Union Parishad enforceable if the relative doesn't pay?

   2. *Complaint to the government.* Under the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013, anyone harmed can complain to the government against any person, not only an agency, for fraud or taking money illegally; it must be investigated within 30 working days and settled within 3 months, and the government can order the money recovered and paid to you <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>. The Ministry's enforcement branch (sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030255) asks for proof, your full address and, for an agency, its RL number, which a relative doesn't have <sup>[91](#o91)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Do the Ministry's enforcement branch or BMET's complaint cell take a complaint against a private person?
      > - Has either recovered money from a private person?

   3. *Police case.* Taking money on a false promise of a job abroad carries up to 5 years and a fine of at least Tk 1 lakh, and buying or selling a visa inside Bangladesh 2 to 7 years <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>. These offences are cognisable, so the police station can register the case, non-bailable and can't be settled by compromise; a magistrate is to finish the trial within 4 months of the charge <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>. A civil suit for compensation can run alongside <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>, and people cheated in the name of migration have a right to reasonable legal aid <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Do police stations register these cases against a relative?
      > - What does a lawyer charge in a district court?

   4. *From Qatar,* complain against the employer to MOL and the Embassy's labour wing (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid-are-abused-or-your-passport-is-taken), are abused or your passport is taken, item 2; If you aren't paid, are abused or your passport is taken, item 8) <sup>[N85](#n85), [92](#o92)</sup>. A worker on a free visa who works elsewhere has no legal recourse if unpaid <sup>[51 (2021)](#o51)</sup>.

<a id="step-2-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates"></a>

### 3.3 Step 2: Passport, police clearance and certificates

Get these ready while the employer applies for the visa: QVC won't book an appointment without a passport with enough validity, and MOI's visa needs a police clearance and attested certificates <sup>[84](#o84), [8](#o8)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-2-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-1"></a>**Get a passport valid for more than 195 days.** QVC books appointments only for passports with more than 195 days' validity <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>; with 195 days or less, renew it and tell your sponsor the new number before booking <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>.

   You'll need: your original NID; applicants over 20 must use the NID <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>.

   1. Fill in the e-passport application exactly as on your NID <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>.
   2. Pay online through A-Challan (the government's online payment-slip system) or the online payment gateways DGePay or ShurjoPay (by card, bKash or Nagad), or at any bank through A-Challan <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>. A 48-page e-passport for 5 years costs Tk 4,025 (regular), Tk 6,325 (express) or Tk 8,625 (super express); for 10 years Tk 5,750, Tk 8,050 or Tk 10,350, VAT included <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.
   3. Bring the original NID to the passport office <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>. Delivery after biometrics is within 15 working days (regular), 7 (express) or 2 (super express) <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.

   Names sometimes differ between the passport, the QVC appointment letter and the medical papers; workers are told in practice to report a mismatch at QVC reception <sup>[N86 (2022)](#n86)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How do you correct a name spelling <sup>[N86 (2022)](#n86)</sup>?
   > - What does QVC or MOI do when the visa name differs from the passport <sup>[N86 (2022)](#n86)</sup>?
